ParlaMint-ES_2022-12-21-CD221221.u198	Thank you so much. For the Basque Parliamentary Group, Mrs Gorospe Elezcano has the floor. [[EAJ-PNV]]
ParlaMint-ES_2022-12-21-CD221221.u199	Thank you, President. Good morning, minister. Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. This law is not our law, it is not the law of our university system, of the Basque university system, but we will vote in favour because there has been a very broad work. I would like to thank you for the work that has been done by your team for years and for the willingness to agree that you have manifested and demonstrated throughout all these years, both under the leadership of former Minister Castells and with you, Minister Subirats. I want to thank Marta Cruells and Aniza Garcia for their work. As a result of this work, the text that is being put to the vote today offers some very significant progress and, above all, does not prevent the future normative development that our university systems require in order to be able to advance and improve with ambition, which is what we intend to do. Therefore, from the beginning our request was for the law to be a general law, a framework law, that would allow within it to give way and enable this normative development of the different university systems that exist. We are keeping ten amendments alive, which I take for granted, and I would like - in these five minutes that I have - to be able to explain to you quickly, as a decalogue, ten important aspects which have been incorporated into the amendment phase and which are very important. Firstly, something very important for Basque territory, which is on both sides - in the Spanish State and in the French State -. The autonomous communities, connected with other European regions through the Euroregions, are being strengthened in the field of cross-border cooperation. This is very important for Euskadi, Navarra and Iparralde students. In this way, the autonomous communities participating in Euroregions will be able to promote the automatic recognition of studies that facilitate access to the university. Secondly, the university sphere is a reference point in language standardization through the promotion of official languages in the territories. The text strongly reflects that the promotion of the language of the Autonomous Communities is not limited to dissemination and learning, but primarily to its use as a language of academic transmission. Thirdly, the evaluation agencies of the Autonomous Communities are moving towards the perspective of European agencies with full competence, strengthening their accreditation and evaluation functions. Before the amendments tabled, these agencies of the autonomous communities had limited their functions to the local level in a nuclear field of university policy such as that of personnel policy. In the future, they extend their competence to the accreditation of all teaching and research personnel, both in the field of the workforce, which they already had, and in the field of civil servants. What's new: this situation enables workers to enjoy valid accreditation for mobility between university systems. Fourthly, governance and democracy within the university are strengthened, so that permanent staff can be expanded in universities, recognizing the ability of this collective to access other levels of governance, for example, to gain access to the position of greater responsibility and rank in the university, that of rector or rector. Fifthly, the explicit mention of dual training, a reality implanted in the Basque universities, which was ignored in the text, is introduced. The Basque strategy University + Enterprise is recognized through the explicit mention of dual training in law, a training initiative promoted from Euskadi and that has been the subject of multiple political initiatives both in Congress and in the Senate by our Basque Parliamentary Group [[EAJ-PNV]] Sixthly, autonomous powers are advanced and strengthened, both by setting the maximum prices for public prices and for the scholarship and study aid programme. Changes are also incorporated to respect the budgetary powers of the Autonomous Communities. The capacity of the General Conference on University Policy to set ceilings on public prices is eliminated. The exclusive reference to the State as the sole guarantor of equal opportunities in access to university is eliminated through the system of grants and grants, while recognizing the competence of the autonomous communities in this area from their own budgets. Seventhly, greater autonomy of the Social Council is being promoted, both in the budgetary and policy fields, to support the progress of universities towards excellence and its impact on society. Eighthly, in the context of lifelong learning, which is included in the draft law, persons who do not have any university qualifications enabling them to access continuing training qualifications and who are able to demonstrate professional or professional experience with a level of competence equivalent to university academic training will be able to access continuing education by means of a procedure for the recognition of professional experience, which is very important in Euskadi, where we have a great connection between the enterprise, vocational training and the university, and persons who have acquired their knowledge and training in the field of not always university and in the field of vocational training and enterprise. Ninth, the door is opened so that private non-profit universities, declared of social interest, can also participate in public programmes to promote research and technological development. And, in the tenth place, the guarantee of autonomous competition. Both university autonomy and autonomous competences are safeguarded, establishing a clear separation of the competences of each party and an integral vision for the harmonious development of university systems and universities. The capacity of the autonomous communities to coordinate universities located in their area of competence is explicitly recognized. As I said, this is not our law, but will we find at some other time the capacity and the possibility of adopting an organic law and having a majority that incorporates all these positive aspects?

ParlaMint-ES_2022-12-21-CD221221.u201	Thank you, Mr. President. Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. Good morning, Minister. Ladies and gentlemen, I would like to say that we have achieved the best possible law in universities, but unfortunately we believe that it is not. We would like to say that we have achieved a university model that guarantees plurality and tolerance, that ensures an environment of freedom so that all ideological positions have their space to be defended and debated and where the word is always imposed on force, but it is not so. We would like to be glad that we have agreed on a university system where we can have the opportunity to study in any university or to work and research in any university, but we do not think that is the case either. We would like to say that we have finally achieved a model of excellence that is fundamentally committed to society in order to train future generations in the best possible way and to ensure that the university exits into a labour market, with the certainty that access to that labour market is made in an agile and flexible way, to a demanding, specialized and constantly evolving reality, but unfortunately we do not believe that this is the case either. Finally, we would like to think that we have reformed and improved the university's governance system by making it more effective and more agile, and that will not be the case. And I sincerely say this with regret, even with a certain personal anger, because the Spanish university does not deserve to be a toy used for political and party purposes by those who do not know and do not respect precisely its character as universal, as headquarters and as a source of knowledge and as a tool of the argument. Unfortunately, the university, like so many other institutions - and we have seen it recently - we believe that it is again being manipulated by politics and that, ladies and gentlemen, I think it is a failure if we assume that we are here to make the best law possible and we have not been able to do so, but it is an even greater failure if we have not voluntarily wanted to make the best law possible. It is true that in Citizens we had bet on this law. In fact, we looked forward to some of the initial issues, which have been disappearing from the various texts raised as the demands in the Government of Unity Podemos and the nationalist partners of this Government have been increasing. We have tabled 67 of the 803 amendments to the bill. Some of them are in the opinion, others have been negotiated, but we have been rejected amendments which we consider to be fundamental and essential for the real and in-depth reform of the Spanish university system, and we believe that this is still the great outstanding subject. We have also seen the acceptance of other amendments which we do not consider necessary or appropriate - even unacceptable - because they are not in the objective of truly reforming the university system. We maintain our amendments 288 to 292 to regulate the guarantee of a true evaluation of the quality of the university system, since we believe that we cannot pretend that the system works, and works well, without being subject to an ongoing evaluation, with criteria approved by the General Conference on University Policy, ensuring improvement and continuous innovation in that evaluation. But what is more, we believe that this law is the appropriate framework - which if not - for defining the evaluation procedure, clearly determining both its object and its characteristics. Its object is nothing other than to identify perfectly the university quality with the professional orientation and the necessary adaptation of training programmes and accreditation of competences, because we can no longer take out on the street promotions of graduates who then find themselves with a labor and professional market that has nothing or has very little to do with the knowledge acquired. As for the characteristics of the system's evaluation, it must be linked to the principles of continuity, objective and permanent improvement, on the basis of pre-established indicators, the plurality of sources of verification or the submission to basic criteria for the evaluation of public policies, such as relevance, effectiveness, efficiency, social impact and sustainability, and none of this is observed in the article. Moreover, we propose that a report be made on the state of the university system, so that society as a whole and public agents, as well as the university community itself, can know the general state of the system at first hand periodically; a report that talks about the supply of university degrees, employability, the needs of the public and private sectors and the adequacy to that demand. These amendments have not been accepted. We also maintain our amendment 295 because we really want to bet on real internationalisation. To this end, it is necessary to speed up the comparability of foreign degrees, either by automatic equivalence of degree and master's degree obtained in a country of the European Union, or by that of the doctorate obtained in any country member of the European Higher Education Area or in countries approved by international conventions. We maintain our amendments 314 to 219, where we propose that in the area of governance specific management competencies be set up in order to be able to take up the post of rector, as well as that those who aspire to be present a draft direction of objectives and purposes, of resources and means for this, and how it proposes to evaluate its role. We believe that the rector of a university can perfectly be an expert professional in management of both the private and public sectors, since in his duties as such he neither investigates nor imparts teaching, so it is necessary to break outdated structures and to bet on a much more professional and effective management and governance. Therefore, we also propose that the statutes of universities may provide for the election of its rector through a specific body for this purpose, in which persons outside the university of recognized academic, cultural, social, business or institutional prestige, local, national or international, as well as teaching and research staff, students and technical staff in management, administration and services. We bet in our amendments on a single-person body choice model that makes it possible to set alternative systems based on merit and capacity both in management and in research and teaching, and we propose to allow teachers to participate in companies that have R & D collaborations with universities, even if universities themselves do not participate in those societies, entities or consortia in question, as we have managed to do in the research field with the new science law. We also keep alive our amendment 327 because we assume the autonomous reality in the university sphere and the existence and work of autonomous quality agencies, together with ANECA, but we believe it is absolutely essential that the evaluation and accreditation criteria be uniform and uniform precisely in order to guarantee equality and mobility throughout Spain. And finally, we believe it is necessary to strengthen the institutional neutrality of the university, something which belongs to it by its very nature, but which, unfortunately, must constantly be remembered by the courts of justice. There is one very important issue on which we have already touched: funding. At the moment our amendment 323 has not been accepted to raise this funding, in the area of university research, to 5% of its budget in universities until 1% of GDP in education is reached in 2030.

ParlaMint-ES_2022-12-21-CD221221.u211	Good afternoon, deputies and deputies. Minister, with this law we had a challenge, the challenge of thinking about the future, of ensuring the survival of the university as a central element of democratization, research, critical thinking and transfer of knowledge to the environment. We had to move away from the previous legislative framework of 2001 in order to draw a new university framework, a framework that for our parliamentary group should be as basic as possible for everyone to develop their model according to their circumstances. Seeing that the majority in the negotiation of this law passed through the Catalan and Basque parties, we wanted to bet on inciting. And although at the beginning of the negotiation - three years ago - the text proposed must be said to be inconsumable, today we can say that we have managed to make this law an improvement over the previous law. With the inclusion of some 150 amendments throughout the long process, Esquerra Republicana and the Government of the Republic have succeeded in promoting a public university of quality, democratic and accessible. [[Applauds]] So far, every time the student wanted to strike, he had to fight with the university's government team, and it wasn't always possible to reach agreements, much less to be able to match the studies with activism. When this law is passed, for the first time in history the student's right to strike, called the right to academic unemployment, will be recognized. And when this happens, the student will have guaranteed his or her right to make the studies compatible with student associationism. They will have a law that strengthens them in these constant battles. A right will be adopted to enable the student to obtain new rights. Today, in most Catalan universities, students have difficulty doing classes in Catalan, even if the curriculum itself establishes this. For an idea to be made, in many degrees we are talking about 25% in Catalan - so much so that they like this percentage - not other languages, and without having two language options. When this law is passed, the student will have the right to know in his or her curricula the language of instruction of the subject, which will enable him or her to demand compliance with the agreed provisions. And when this happens, the government will have to boost mobility programmes, such as Erasmus +, in official languages other than Spanish. And research and transfer in the official languages will be encouraged. In addition, funding for universities in these languages will be increased so that they can develop plans to boost these languages. So far, the PDI and PAS hired did not have the same rights and duties as the civil servant, despite the fact that some universities have opted for this type of model. When this law is passed, for the first time, rights and duties will be equated between workers and civil servants, to the extent that, from now on, contractors may also be principals of a university. And when this happens, within one year the ANECA will have to develop agreements for the recognition of autonomous agencies, such as HERE. In addition, agencies such as the AQU may even accredit officials. This law will recognize for the first time the Catalan or Basque model and, despite the limits established by the State itself, will make it inevitable that the working model will continue to develop. So far, most of the associate faculty did not want to be associate faculty; they had a job outside the university to have a foot inside the university, with the precariousness that all this implies. When this law is passed, we will start to put a solution to the precariousness of this teacher, preventing that figure from being used to reduce costs. And when this happens, it will also begin to solve the precariousness of the entire associated IDP, driving stabilization processes, without taking into account the replacement rate and based on the reform that was made of EBEP. Until now, private universities of a single branch and without any quality were allowed to be set up, provided that the autonomous community allowed them to do so; the case of Madrid, for example, will ring a bell. When this law is passed, it will be defined what the university should be. And when this happens, they will increase the quality requirements of the private and will be forced to have university advocates. The least we have to do with the private is to require them to make universities, not lobbies. Until now, universities such as the University of Barcelona or the Polytechnic University of Catalonia were reported to the courts for positioning themselves on issues of deep political relevance and impact in their environment. When this law is passed, universities will be able to analyse and discuss topical topics by law. It is enough to limit freedom of expression; universities must be and are democratic pillars and must be able to exercise as such. But in addition to everything we have said, we have also succeeded in ensuring that the law does not prejudice the situation we had previously. With our negotiation we have preserved the Catalan selection model, the Serra Húnter model of excellence and the competences of universities and self-governments, and in some cases we have even expanded them; also, the models of autonomous scholarships, competition in public prices and models of equality and non-discrimination units; we have managed to allow psychologists and migrant psychologists to approve their qualifications; we have left the door open to specific universities, such as the Universitat de les Arts, which we support from Catalonia; we have ensured that the student can combine his studies with work activity, among many other issues. And yet they know that this can never be our law. If it were, there wouldn't be a king signing titles and most of the staff would be staff members and we wouldn't have a replacement rate. That's what we're working on, making independence possible. But we have a clear conscience that it has been necessary to make it inevitable that in the future they will stop blocking the work model or that they will stop putting limits on the generational relevancy of the university. We end with one warning: today there is one stage, but another begins to follow up on the agreements reached, and for this we will be especially alert that this law is accompanied by a substantial increase in State funding. This depends on whether this legislative change has an impact. They're carrying out their responsibility. Thank you. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2022-12-21-CD221221.u213	President, ladies and gentlemen, Minister, you see that when the independence and separatist groups want to improve a national law it is precisely in order to further deepen their quest for independence; you know who the rooms are playing with. [[Applauds]] You will know that the university is a Latin-rooted concept that points to what is universal precisely - that is why it is called university and not particularity - that has its core in Greek schools, in Aristotelian high school or in the Platonic academy and, as a matter of essence, it is basically the search for truth, this search for truth that the Greeks called philosophy, the search for knowledge. These schools also developed during the Roman period, the Proculletana and Sabinian schools of Roman law. Sabiniana has nothing to do with the racist and xenophobic founder of the Basque Nationalist Party and not with Joaquín Sabina. [[Applauds]] And then the universalizing explosion of medieval universities led by scholastics, the University of Paris, the University of Salamanca, the University of Coimbra, to the present universities, especially the Anglo-Saxons, where the most innovative sciences and technologies are studied. ParlaMint-ES_2022-12-21-CD221221.u227	Good afternoon, everyone. I would simply like to highlight a few things at this end of the debate. I think this law was absolutely necessary. The former, the Organic Law of Universities, has been in force for more than twenty-one years. It was done at a time when all the powers of the autonomous communities were still to be deployed. It was done at a time when technological changes of all kinds that have been taking place in recent years were unimaginable. We therefore understood that it was absolutely essential to deal with the reform that this law poses of the entire university system. In order to make this reform, which my predecessor Manuel Castells has been rightly said to have started, in this year that I have been in office, I as a minister and the team of the Ministry of Universities have carried out a very broad consultation of all sectors of the university community. I have visited more than thirty public and private universities directly on their campuses. We have held many meetings and days of reflection and debate with all the universities. We have had more than one hundred and fifty meetings with all the social partners, with the collegiate bodies. I would also like to point out the meeting I had with former ministers and former ministers of universities, such as Pilar del Castillo, Mercedes Cabrera and Ángel Gabilondo, and with representatives of student and trade union groups, and obviously with parliamentary groups. I believe that this is an important and important work over time and that this project which is now under discussion has been improving during this process. I think it is a process, therefore, of intensive dialogue, of building consensus, which ultimately means that the law can contribute to the university system facing these next twenty years at least. If, as I hope, in this final procedure today and tomorrow the law continues, by the decision of all of you, I believe that we will finally have a law that improves the current situation. Why? Because it recognizes the diversity and plurality of the university system and, at the same time, incorporates new elements that are significant; for example, the whole process of Europeanisation of the university system, which I believe should be highlighted. Europe's higher education policies are no longer international policy, they are internal policy. They will increasingly mark the functioning of this system and I believe that the law responds to that criterion. It also surpasses the effects of the 2008 economic crisis and under-financing - which has already been mentioned - and marks that 1% of GDP horizon, which I believe is significant, by 2030. It eliminates the precariousness, which was one of the consequences of this under-financing, reducing the temporality from 40% to 8% and, at the same time, proposes to respond to the ageing of staff by encouraging stabilization and promotion programmes. It democratises access to the university, as has already been said, by establishing a system of scholarships as a subjective right linked to the socio-economic situation of the applicants and, at the same time, raises the containment and reduction of public prices, which, instead, was not done in the previous period, because the universities' response was precisely to raise the fees. It strengthens the university's connection with society through various mechanisms. I would also like to highlight here the issue of lifelong learning, with micro-credits, the subject of open science and the development of citizen science. It increases the degree of internationalization of the university system, favoring that students do not have to apply for a residence permit every year, but that they can do so for the duration of their studies for another year; I think this is quite important. And, finally, it guarantees, I believe, the quality standards of the Spanish university system with very significant quality requirements, both in teaching and in research and transfer. I would therefore like to thank everyone for the effort to reach this point. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2022-12-21-CD221221.u230	Thank you so much. For the Basque Parliamentary Group, Mr Barandiaran Benito has the floor. [[EAJ-PNV]]
ParlaMint-ES_2022-12-21-CD221221.u231	Good afternoon, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen, Minister, on, on, on, on. Let's start by introducing the regulatory framework, Minister. Employment as such is not one of the related matters in article 149 of the Constitution, nor is it in the Statute of Self-Government of the Vasco Country. However, it has always been understood as a cross-cutting matter and the Constitutional Court itself has stated this, referring to the following titles in order to be able to intervene in it: if it is in the field of work, it is based on 149.1.7, and if it is in the overall planning of economic activity, 149.1.13. In addition, the Constitutional Court has also stated that the mobilization of financial resources intended to regulate the labour market and full employment is not limited to what is being understood as a strict sensu labour regime, but to measures affecting the labour market as a whole, in compliance with the guideline of article 40 of the Constitution on the formulation or obtaining of full employment. As far as the Basque Country is concerned, Articles 10, 25 and 12.2 of the Statute of Autonomy empower the Autonomous Community of the Basque Country to establish and develop its own employment policy in accordance with the general management of economic activity, but, as far as the latter is concerned, it is understood that it can also exercise legislative powers in matters which are not strictly labour-related but which are related to them in order to engage in active employment policies, in job creation, in the promotion of employment which, as a matter other than that of work itself, can also be linked to economic development and economic activity. There is also - and I say this as a counter-position - that a preliminary draft Basque employment law is already being published and pending access to the Basque Parliament. It is a preliminary draft that substantially coincides with the objectives and measures of this State employment law, but it also provides for an organization and structure of its public services, in the exercise of its powers of self-organization, which first address the existence of own policies that are the consequence of the transfer, through Royal Decree 1441 of 2010, of functions and services in the implementation of labour legislation in the field of employment, employment and vocational training for employment; and, secondly, address the existence of an own body, Lanbide, the Basque Employment Service, which was created by law as such. In addition, it follows that this preliminary draft Basque employment law provides that public employment policies in Euskadi have as their primary objective the realization of the right to decent, decent work, where the regulatory framework must have as its purpose the policies of promoting employment and extending social protection. For this reason, and without prejudice to the participation of identical and similar principles, structure and functions, we propose at the moment an amendment to the whole, which after a process of dialogue was withdrawn, and then partial or particular amendments that have been aimed at clarifying those aspects of the draft law that could affect the exercise of those own policies that are developed in the exercise of the competences that were already transferred and assumed by the Autonomous Community of the Basque Country. In them we pointed out that we wanted participation in the different State bodies to be carried out in any case as a collaboration between public administrations, but public administrations equally competent in this field. For this reason, different amendments of our group concerning employment policy objectives, the powers of employment policy planning, the State Employment Agency, the governance of the system, the Spanish Employment Agency, i.e. all those referred to in Title II in the organization of the activity and the system of employment of the State have been dealt with. I would like to highlight among the amendments that we have proposed two, which have been dealt with by your ministry. One has been an additional provision 11th new - that is to say - which refers not only to the fact that the exercise of the powers provided for in this law will be exercised for Euskadi, in accordance with the legal regime governing that transfer of Royal Decree 1441, but, and in particular - and this is important - in accordance with its link to the economic concert, which acquires special significance in the definition and development of employment policies, not only because of the origins of the economic resources allocated to them, which some would have to clarify in order to make it known that they do not come from the distribution of funds agreed at the Sectoral Employment Conference, but also because it contains a broad approach to the subject which allows us to participate from their point of view. And there is another amendment concerning the important titles of competition, the distinction that I have just made between 149.1.7 and 149.1.3. In other words, it allowed us to participate in a specific way in our own policies on economic development and economic activity planning. In short, we have finally understood that as a result of these agreements, this bill was already within the scope of the agreement, it was also within the scope of equality of objectives, purposes and means, including economic sufficiency for all employment activity already covered by the Basque employment law, which already covers Lanbide's activity and we hope that in the future it will also be able to guarantee this law for the whole State. We have therefore understood that certain levels of agreement have been reached in their development that allow their support, but on the understanding that this activity or any other activity in which we are going to be involved in the field of collaboration involves collaboration and respect between public administrations equally competent in this field. It is not recreated in past precariousness, but it projects stability and opportunities for tomorrow. It brings us closer to Europe, in figures, to finding employment, to public policies and to evaluating them - we are going from being the exception of the European Union to being a reference - and basically involves an approach to employability and a structural transformation of active employment, decent and quality employment policies at the centre, because poor employment is always a bad job. I'm going to tell you an anecdote. Whenever I hear the word "employability", I remember a professor of economics who was chairman of the Employment Commission, Alberto Montero. He said that every time we talked about employability, a kitten died, and he said it because in Spain this concept was only used to criminalize the workers, to send them a hard and false message, which is basically that if you don't have a job, it's your fault. This changes with this law. The message from now on is another one. Employment and unemployment are not isolated episodes, they are not meteorological events, they are parts of a vital and professional trajectory that must be of quality. We link employability to training and personal accompaniment, without criminalizing, taking on public responsibilities. We talk about a catalogue of guaranteed services and individual itineraries to achieve complete career paths. Workers are entitled to a common service portfolio, regardless of where they reside. Each person will have an individualized digital profile, a personalized itinerary and will receive tutoring throughout his/her working life. This orientation incorporates for the first time entrepreneurs and self-employed, a fundamental part of our country. That's how we believe and that's how we do it. The public, digitised, modernised Spanish Employment Agency is set up, which will be multidisciplinary in nature with new governance and will manage this catalogue of services. This law involves a transformation into mediation, to make it more accessible, more efficient and with a single employment portal, with the obligation to notify vacancies, which is a great novelty. The new law entails a far-reaching transformation in active employment policies. The local dimension is strengthened in response to the specific needs of each territory, with particular attention to crisis and relocation processes. This country's employment policies have failed in coordination with the local level, and this standard incorporates this perspective in its own governance. It eliminates gaps, in this the law focuses: the digital divide, the gender gap, the generational gap, the education gap and also the territorial gap. This law puts us back on the job as pioneers in Europe. And you know something funny? It has been validated by the seventeen autonomous communities and their corresponding public employment services, yes, also those governed by the People's Party. In Congress they say this is the worst, but in the communities where they govern they validate them. Who lies, ladies and gentlemen, to the autonomous governments or their national deputies? This law provides three thousand job guides and professionalizes this collective that so good work does to achieve quality itineraries. And I wonder: What is the People's Party going to do in the communities it governs? They have been refusing to consolidate the jobs of these workers for years. Are you going to reject these professionals? And the question is relevant, because let me give you an example: in Castile and León, the People's Party and VOX are returning the funds of the social partners involved in collective bargaining; they say they work for Spain, but at the slightest opportunity they leave the workers in this country unprotected. In Castile and León, the People's Party and VOX have just eliminated the funds for the system of mediation of conflicts in companies, the bodies that must avoid conflictivity and that serve basically to prolong conflicts. You do not defend the working class or the interests of companies; you want mud and fight not only in Congress, but also in companies. In Castile and León, the People's Party and VOX are going to withdraw funds that allow workers' legal representatives to train in occupational safety. There are women workers who die in their jobs and you mutilate the funds that also serve to avoid it. Are you going to do the same with this law? We will continue to expand rights and guarantees, because this law establishes new priority groups; it combats stereotypes and gaps in all its actions and aspires to effective and real equality, which is why the potential beneficiaries are expanded in this case: women victims of gender-based violence, people in situations of social exclusion or the group of people who have abandoned employment services. People over the age of 45 also fall into this category, which was about time. An important and novel aspect is that it introduces, inter alia, policy evaluation. We will therefore be able to understand which active policies work and which do not, because we basically create three new rates: intermediation, employability and coverage. We must therefore have a good system of evaluation of public policies and there will be specific obligations to measure the effectiveness and efficiency of actions. Finally, there are two amendments that I would like to highlight: that of asbestos, which in this case expedites the recognition of benefits, ensures that victims are paid and that they receive what is theirs, and that of the Labour Inspectorate, which will finally have that capacity and authority for the administrative authorization of EREs. Ladies and gentlemen, we have made more progress in labour stability over the past year than in the past thirty-seven, and we will continue to do so. As long as you run through the expired courts to bring down what you cannot here, we will continue to work to make this country more dignified, fairer and more democratic, even if it fucks you. Merry Christmas. [[Applauds]]

An opportunity is lost to offer LGBT people and people through comprehensive legislation, guaranteed legislation, legislation that addresses the problems faced by this group of people and also strengthens the exercise of their rights. The government of PSOE and Podemos has preferred to arrogate the representativeness of trans people, who are not the property of any party, and have transferred to the text of their law - we must be aware - internal disputes. This, on issues of equality - I have said it many times - is absolutely not conducive to the social response. It has been quickly legislated, with no consensus, with also lack of legal rigour in this House, in parliamentary activity. I have to say this is the first time I've faced a paper the same day it opens closes. That is how we have worked on this legislative framework, and I think it is important to say so, because I believe that legal rigour also deserves working time. As a parliamentarian I must say that I do not agree with the process that has been used for the work of a law that is necessary, that has to be very well worked out and that, moreover, it must reflect socially consensus, agreement and good doing. There are many flaws that have been made, as I say, until we get here, but there is one that is flagrant, and it is its sluggish processing. It seems to me that, being one of the main laws of our democracy, it does not deserve a sluggish treatment. In addition, I do not think there is any justification for avoiding certain parliamentary procedures, for overstepping the rights of the opposition or for twisting the Rules of Procedure of this House, which is what you, ladies and gentlemen of the PSOE and ladies and gentlemen of Podemos have done. I think it is important that we talk about the need to address the reality of LGBT people and trans people. I think it is very important to take into account what your real needs are. The Citizens' Parliamentary Group is aware of what its real needs are. I believe that we are faced with an important advance in rights when the LGBTI and trans people are depathized once and for all. He believed that one of the main recognitions we had to make was depathology. We've always agreed. We have always been in agreement with gender self-determination, we have always been in agreement that trans women at no time left a single woman. Of course we are aware of the progress in rights that this legislation entails, of course we see it, but I also believe that legislation could be more secure, and that is where the amendments that the Citizens' Parliamentary Group put forward pointed out. We bet on a guarantee in the procedure itself for the recognition of gender self-determination in the Civil Registry. We wanted to improve it, we wanted to take into account greater protection of the situation of minors, more information to parents. But it has been impossible in this process of processing to be able to speak, to be able to dialogue, to arrive at consensual positions that, in the end, bring legal rigour to the norm, which I believe is very relevant at the moment we are here. Another of our amendments concerned the area of health care. We wanted to end the difference that existed between one and the other autonomous communities so that everyone would have the same rights throughout the Spanish territory. What we wanted was for the interterritorial council to agree on a protocol, an action that unifies and guarantees the rights of LGBTI and transgender people throughout the territory. But you didn't want to hear it either. Similarly, we are committed to specific regulation in the field of sport. Nor was it intended to listen; moreover, we have not even been able to work on it with the procedure that has been put in place. Trans people deserve guaranteed legislation, driven by people who don't shake their wrists when dealing with issues of vital importance to our citizens. Trans people need to be demonstrated in the field of unemployment, because they are among the groups with the greatest social rejection and suffer greater discrimination in the field of employment. We are aware that there are actions that we need to improve. Furthermore, we believe that this proposal should have been offered to society, not as a confrontation of those who carry this kind of flag within the Government itself, not as a confrontation and without almost any dialogue. I think that, in the causes of equality, weak favor we do - I praise on more than one occasion - if we do not offer a unified and cohesive look at our society, because not everyone in our society is sensitive to the causes, and parliamentary reflection is very important. I believe that we, where we have ruled, in Madrid or Andalusia, have always promoted guaranteed legislation on LGBTI and trans. Therefore, there is no doubt that we are in favour of the progress that this legislation represents, but not of the legal abuse that is regulated in this House. We believe that this legislation should be improved, and we are not being heard, we are not being heard from the rest of the parliamentary groups. It is therefore a bitter sweet taste because, on the one hand, I believe that there is an advance in recognition of rights, but, on the other hand, I believe that neither the House nor the Ministry nor the Government have given the answer that this just cause deserved. ParlaMint-ES_2022-12-21-CD221221.u303	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, excuse this delay. [[The lady]] This good predisposition, although it should be the general tonic and the norm in this house, is unfortunately not present in all commissions nor emanates from all ministries. As a result of this good predisposition, we have succeeded in ensuring that, between the paper and the work of the Commission, this rule which we are debating today comes out of Congress better than what came in. For citizens, any reform of securities market regulation must be based on three pillars. Firstly, in the pillar of transparency. One of the main risks in dealing with financial assets and market investment is that we talk about very complex elements, which sometimes makes it difficult to make an accurate assessment of the risks of investing your money in one place or another, a high risk, especially when we talk about small savers. This is why ensuring market transparency is essential to enable all investments to be made with the greatest possible knowledge of their nature and the risks associated with them. Secondly, any regulation must be based on dynamism; a securities market must be dynamic, must be stimulating and must be attractive for it to be ready to participate in it. To this end, their regulation must be rigorous enough to ensure the transparency that we previously spoke of, but also flexible enough to allow the emergence of new types of business models or types of assets that can respond to the needs that are emerging in the Spanish economy. [[The Lord]] Moreover, it must be a market that can look at the stock markets of the countries in our environment on an equal footing. Only being a dynamic market will be able to do so. Thirdly, any regulation of securities markets must foster the momentum of business growth, and here we find our greatest outstanding task. The European business fabric, and especially the Spanish one, are characterized by an excessive dependence on the banking sector for its financing, which, given its lower propensity to take risks, can be a major entry barrier for thousands of companies that, if they obtained initial bank financing, would be viable. This barrier is even more important in the case of innovative small businesses. These, only these, have been our master lines of work throughout the processing of this bill which is very complex and very technical in several respects. As I said at the beginning, many of these characteristics created doubts when starting work, but, thanks to the negotiation, we have reached a consensus and have been able to resolve them. During the work of the Commission and the Commission, an important issue that has been discussed has been the role of financial advisers and their adherence to FOGAIN, or we have also discussed the better incorporation and regulation of distributed registers. However, this has been resolved. I have to defend here some amendments that have remained pending and that I bring to the House and I would like you to reflect on them and on your voting position. We have two amendments for this final debate. One, to give greater autonomy and independence to the National Securities Market Commission, because we want to provide it with a staff system that suits its needs. It is a historical request, I might say, of the organism that follows. It is not subject to the law regulating it but to the law establishing public employment. However, every year in the State's General Budgets it is provided with a budget that prevents it from making independent decisions on personnel matters. It is true that autonomy, also from the control of the Executive, must be guaranteed to this body in order to give greater legal certainty to our financial market, something always desirable in any public body, but essential in the light of the last acts of the Government, just with the CNMV, because it is only worth remembering the case of Indra: they warned the president by shortening his mandate and threatened to change the law. Another issue of the amendments that we are defending and that we have proposed again, as we have been doing for the past two years, is the development of an active Spanish crypto strategy and a high-level cryptoactive forum. Recent developments in the world of this type of asset, cryptos, show that this strategy is more necessary than ever to enhance the development of this type of innovation in Spain in the most secure and transparent way possible. In short, they are amendments that will improve this text and that will come out with the broad consensus that I have been talking about so far in this House.

As regards very specific insurance, the unit linked, establishes an asymmetry with respect to the remuneration that can be provided to insurance agents and insurance brokers. What this amendment seeks to do is to normalise this situation and that the remuneration to the insurance brokers can be done in the same way, that is, through the companies and through the insurance agents. Therefore, with your vote and with the permission of the Constitutional Court, I intend to approve this amendment. Thank you very much and good afternoon.

The preamble states that the existence of a transparent and efficient securities market is one of the basic elements of any developed and advanced social market economy. It plays an essential role in allocating financial resources to the economic sectors, investing in productive assets, promoting public savings and opening up the economy externally and integrating it into international financial and trade flows, and a regulatory framework is needed to help build stable, transparent and efficient financial markets and ensure the highest standards of protection for financial services customers and savers. The importance of this law and of this bill is therefore clear. A framework law that aggregates all the changes of the last decade required in most cases from the European Union, which between the consequences of the last financial crisis and the progress and competition of international capital markets has required multiple reforms; reforms that have not yet been completed and that this law will help to integrate. ParlaMint-ES_2022-12-21-CD221221.u317	President, ladies and gentlemen, good afternoon to all and everyone. Today we are discussing an extensive, highly complex legislative text, as has already been said, which aims to transpose into national law a number of directives from the European Parliament and the Council. To begin with, I would like to highlight, as has also been done again by other rapporteurs, the proven will of the Ministry of Economic Affairs to open a process of dialogue, not only with the parliamentary groups called to approve and eventually amend and matify this law, but also with different voices of civil society, whose perspectives have tried to incorporate as much as possible into the body and spirit of this law. For example, issues relating to the Investment Guarantee Fund, FOGAIN, which covers fraud, the impossibility of recovering securities deposited in an investment services company up to a maximum of EUR 100 000, and to which all investment services companies, including financial advisory bodies, will now have to adhere. Well, during the appearance phase that was reserved for this processing, the main representative associations of these entities transferred to us their concerns related to the fact that financial advisory entities, which do not manage money or customer values, but only advise assets, have an obligation to adhere to the fund that they will never use. People such as Mr Ibáñez Ferriol, of the Spanish Association of Financial Advisory Companies, but also others such as Mr Pich Rosell, of the General Council of Colleges of Economists of Spain, or Mrs González de Frutos, of the Spanish Union of Insurance and Reinsurance Entities, in addition to the Secretary of State himself, Mr García Andrés, were able to contribute their views and were fully heard by the Commission on this and other issues covered by the regulations. In any case, it should be recalled that FOGAIN also covers cases where an unauthorized entity for the management of funds and assets acts fraudulently or unlawfully, i.e. the measure has its technical and legal motivation in our view. Another thing is that the minimum contribution required is what it really is, and here we understand that it can result in the unfeasibility of some companies, as I think Mr Paniagua also said. That is why we assume that this extreme must be corrected, so that there is proportionality according to the type of service that is provided, the size of the company, etc. In general, we believe that the present text substantially improves the law of 2015 to the extent that it corrects some elements that have been proved useless or obsolete since then. For example, first, skip the legislative technique of the Securities Market Act by transforming it into a real framework law while containing only those basic principles and main elements of securities market regulation. Secondly, the bill simplifies some procedures and eliminates redundant administrative burdens to facilitate attracting investors. We also believe that it improves the governance of the CNMV, which will surely make it more independent. Finally, the adaptation of our system to the advances in digitisation will ensure legal certainty and recognize the usefulness of new technologies in development. On the other hand, we trust that the economic effects of this new rule will lead to greater systemic efficiency. For example, the standard introduces a prudential regime of its own for investment services undertakings which will result in a more effective standard when establishing solvency guarantees for investment services undertakings. The urgent reform of the MiFID 2 Directive has also been incorporated, which will allow for a better flow of business finance through the capital market and thus a greater contribution by the markets to the development of the economy. Similarly, the definition of SME is extended to include companies with debt issues for a period of less than 50 million, when issuers of a minimum amount of EUR 200 million have been envisaged so far. This will increase the possibilities for listing on these markets and improve the company's financing. We are also confident that improvements in the competitiveness of the Spanish securities market will contribute to a better balance in the sources of business finance and, therefore, to a lower dependence on the credit system, which has been the characteristic that has prevailed in the Spanish economic system to date. Finally, specific regulations on the so-called SPACs are introduced with the aim of clarifying their functioning and ensuring that investors can allow the repayment of the capital they invest. The result of all this is a text that will particularly benefit retail or non-professional investors, insofar as they will have sufficient and quality information when they invest and as long as professional intermediaries take care of their interests when they provide these services. Our parliamentary group has always been concerned about the protection of clients of financial services and savers, and it is no less so, since in our country over the last few decades there have been numerous cases of financial scams that have led individuals with a small amount of investment to lose much of their savings, if not all, precisely because of the lack of information and financial knowledge. In this sense, digitisation has made significant progress in bringing investment tools closer to potential investors, which also benefits companies seeking non-bank financing, but at the same time has increased the risk of fraud. An example where we can see it is in the marketing of active cryptos, which has fallen into a young audience and easily captured due to the lack of job opportunities and the high profitability promised in advertising campaigns of this type of products. All this, ladies and gentlemen, tells us that we are on the right track, on the road to orderly regulation of the fair sharing of risks and gains, that is, we put the mechanisms of the market at the service of the people and not under their command. I also take this last minute to thank the Commission for its work, in particular the rapporteurs of this law, of course the President of the Commission, Mr Celso Delgado, it is always a pleasure to be able to go to the committees under your presidency, of course also the lawyer and, moreover, I wish you happy holidays and much luck for next year. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2022-12-21-CD221221.u319	Good afternoon, ladies and gentlemen. I would also like to say good afternoon to the Minister, because I believe that in the debate on a bill of this importance it should be. We have come to debate, and probably to approve, because you have seen the good climate in the Committee on the Economy always, and with this rule there has been in a very special way, the draft of a new law on the securities market, which would be the third in the history of Spain. The first, of 1988, has provided very good services to society, to the nation, and has arrived here in some way because, as you know, it is part of the current one, which is a consolidated text, the Royal Legislative Decree 4/2015. The text we are proposing now, as Mr Capdevila has reminded us, involves the transposition into Spanish law of five directives from the European Parliament and the Council. The last, in fact, was approved on the 14th, so we have not only been up to date with the delays that we were having, but we have moved forward, I do not know if from the right, to the European Union. In addition, as stated in the explanatory statement of the project which is now being put to the vote, a number of subjects have been identified that required updating and modernisation, with the aim of improving the competitiveness and attractiveness of Spanish securities markets, enhancing their entrepreneurial financing capacity, always with the maximum guarantees for the investor. This means, moreover, that the law will no longer be the Securities Market Act to receive a new name; it will be the law of the securities market and investment services. I don't intend to bore you with the content. The law is very prolific, has ten titles, 367 articles and 27 provisions of various kinds. However, I would like to draw your attention to the fundamental mission of a financial system, because this law, together with the Law on the Ordination, Supervision and Solvency of Credit Entities, 10/2014, and the Law on the Ordination, Supervision and Solvency of Insurance Entities, 20/2015, constitute the basic regulation of a financial system. The fundamental mission of a financial system is to capture the surplus of so-called surplus economic units, usually mature families, to apply it in favour of deficit economic units, usually young families and businesses, and for a few decades the Administration. This process of fund-raising and implementation requires intensive work by financial intermediaries and has two purposes. One, on the one hand, is to adapt the risk and term preferences of the savers to the investor's financing demands, because they are often opposed preferences, which is known as the process of financial transformation and, on the other hand, to increase as much as possible the flow of savings aimed at productive investment, which is called the process of financial maximization; and all this to achieve the greatest and most efficient allocation of resources available to an economy, in this case the Spanish one. In this sense, the draft law that we are debating today, together with the regulation that I spoke about before the banking and insurance institutions, constitutes a very good regulation of the Spanish financial system, I dare say. However, I wanted to warn you of the risks that exist so that good regulation does not achieve the desired objectives, which are two. First, legal uncertainty and then the application of savings to consumptive expenditure. Ladies and gentlemen, the legal uncertainty in Spain does not derive so much from the actions of our courts - they work reasonably well - not even from the excessive regulation and intervention of many authorities that interfere in business decision-making processes. Nor do I talk to them about the legal uncertainty of changing regulations. I speak to you of the lack of respect for the law, with capital letters, and of the legal uncertainty generated by a State in a situation of constant questioning, as if a teenager in vocational crisis were being treated, although, after the forty, it is difficult to understand this type of behavior. The application of savings to the financing merely of the expenditure that others cannot meet today does not lay the basis for any growth or - I am afraid - that of the recovery of the borrowed capital, at least in terms of the capacity of the purchasing power, i.e. discounted inflation. It makes no sense, gentlemen, for savers to give up current consumption if they are not assured - within what is insurable when we talk about the future - of a real increase in their future consumption. And it cannot ensure such a real increase if capital is not largely devoted to productive investment, i.e. if an efficient allocation of resources is not made. And public debt is not, much less, a productive investment, except in some very small cases. In the last five years the financing capacity of families - and, in part, of businesses - has been, in spite of everything, frankly high. The Spanish have saved almost 200,000 million euros, which have not been enough to finance the nearly 300,000 that public administrations have needed. That is why we have needed another 200,000 from the European Central Bank to meet that deficit of financing for public debt and to reduce our international financial position. This is a good law for regulating securities markets and investment services, which, together with the laws on the management, supervision and solvency of credit institutions and insurers, assure us a good financial system. However, ladies and gentlemen, you have to be careful, because high-end vehicles do not work well in the desert, they serve no purpose. Arrived here, I wish you happy holidays, at which we celebrate Christmas, the birth of the Redeemer, the only one who can redeem us. I wish you the best in the year 2023 in all respects, except the election, and thank you very much. ParlaMint-ES_2022-12-21-CD221221.u321	Thank you, President. Good afternoon, ladies and gentlemen. The coalition government that President Pedro Sánchez has been acquitting a reformist economic policy, which has been accredited since the beginning of his term. This policy has not been delayed even in the context of extraordinary adversities that it has had to face, from the coronavirus pandemic to the effects and consequences of the war in Ukraine. With the same vocation, it has been used in the transposition of European directives, to eliminate the huge package that it found pending transposition upon arrival. The pandemic crisis at international, European and national levels has triggered a supply crisis, with the breakdown of production chains, a shortage of microchips and, since last year, the rise in energy prices, among other factors. For this reason, the Government has been fully engaged in the implementation of a legislative package, which this Congress has adopted, to improve in macroeconomic and microeconomic terms this supply crisis. Thus, reforms concerning the business environment have included the Emerging Business Ecosystem Development Act, as known as the Startups Act, the Business Creation and Growth Act, the Reform of the Competition Act and the entire package of financial support measures since 2020. And now we close the circle with this bill, which allows us to improve the financing and capitalization mechanisms of companies. The draft Securities Markets and Investment Services Act is a good example of these reformist determinations and the transposition of European legislation. This bill not only seeks to transpose many European directives, but also incorporates European regulations, in order to clarify in a single regulation the legal framework, with the consequent improvement of legal certainty in a market so sensitive to uncertainty and insecurity and also transparency, so necessary when we talk about financial values. At the same time, its approval will resolve the competitiveness gradients of Spanish investment services companies vis-à-vis European competitors. The objectives we propose with this law are to promote financing for businesses and to increase the protection of investors, especially retailers. The first objective is to strengthen the role of securities markets in financing the economy, ensuring that investment services contribute to the capitalisation of Spanish companies by incorporating new financing instruments, especially for SMEs. The draft regulation also provides for the reform of the Capital Companies Act to ensure the protection of investors in listed companies for the purpose of acquisition - the SPACs in their acronyms in English -, also enabling the conditions under which the repayment of the capital invested by shareholders in those companies is carried out. To implement the second of the objectives, regulation is modernised and progress is being made in the incorporation of European legislation in order to meet the challenges arising from the digitalisation process. Thus, ladies and gentlemen, the forecasts of the European legislation on active cryptos will apply, so that the CNMV has the powers on investor protection and financial stability in this area. Any financial instrument that is an active crypt represented by distributed registration technology is defined in the scope of the law. Ladies and gentlemen, Directive 2019/2034 on the Prudential Regime of Investment Services Enterprises is also transposed. The rule establishes a prudential supervisory regime for these companies, adapting them to the particularities of their business model, based on the principle of proportionality. The CNMV shall establish an applicable regime based on the size, nature, scale and complexity of the activities of investment services undertakings, and shall also make this regime more flexible for small enterprises without systemic risk, while maintaining investor protection. In short, the draft Law on Securities Markets and Investment Services seeks to improve the financing and capitalization of Spanish companies; it provides for administrative simplification, eliminating redundant procedures to facilitate the attraction of investments; it allows for the establishment of conditions of competitiveness for Spanish companies in this sector, and it comprises in a single text the rules relating to a market that must necessarily be based on legal certainty and transparency. There is no doubt that wholesalers and retailers will welcome this rule. Ladies and gentlemen, in Spain, where the financing of companies is focused on such a banked financial market, a diversification of sources of financing was needed, especially in small and medium-sized enterprises, which in the past crises of 2008 and 2012 already knew the drastic restrictions of financing, and it is not appropriate that this situation should be repeated in the future. Finally, this project has a high level of agreement. I would like to thank the Ministry of Economic Affairs and Digital Transformation for its work with the parliamentary groups, especially the Ministry of State for Economy and Enterprise Support. It is also necessary to mention the excellent performance of the lawyer and the services of this House for the good end of our work and that of the Commission, and also, of course, that of the President of the Commission, Mr Delgado. From my parliamentary group we reaffirm the gratitude for the talent and willingness to dialogue of all the parliamentary groups and rapporteurs who have participated in the discussion of this law. Thanks to all of them for this broad agreement with which we will adopt this law, practically by consensus. Thus, ladies and gentlemen, it is easy to serve the general interest, the common good of the citizens, with a constructive debate, away from the noise, which too often prevents us from understanding. Just as we reject dishonest behaviors, when the dialogue is fruitful it is necessary to record in the Journal of Sessions. Thank you very much, ladies and gentlemen. [[Applauds]]

Therefore, the production of cogeneration has been decreasing, while gas prices have quadrupled, and the fact is that the regulatory formulas applied so far, even with the incorporation of the gas filling mechanism since October, fail to cover the costs of these plants so that they can compete on an equal footing on the market. In addition, cogenerations continue to await the publication of remunerations for the second half of 2022 and for the first half of 2023. And with regard to the former, the salaries of the first half of the year, the fact is that they have meant a reduction of around 30%, when, as we have said, the price of gas has almost doubled over that period. Therefore, these are wages that do not take into account the increase that the cost of gas has been experiencing. And the fact that they do not know in advance the remuneration they will receive means that they do not know what decisions to make, such as, for example, with regard to joining that top of the mechanism, or with regard to investment, not to say that it is very difficult to budget and plan activities with some uncertainty. Therefore, my group has always advocated the restoration of the conditions of cogeneration and the need for proper regulation of the remuneration of the operation for cogeneration plants, which has a remunerative framework of those regulated in order to ensure that fuel costs are covered through the remuneration of the operation in order to compete in terms equivalent to other technologies. Therefore, on the one hand, we propose that the measures provided for in the motion with regard to tax credit and direct aid be applied to the intensive gas industries on the basis of their energy consumption, particularly to cogenerations, which include the blue-collar sector, with some 28 plants, and, on the other hand, that the Government be urged to review in a definitive manner the remuneration for the operation, which is insufficient or inadequate, as a result of the application of the Henry Hub index or price or the desirability of adjusting the reference value of the free allocation of CO2 to its real values, since, in view of the volatility in prices, they have no legal certainty, which has an impact on production and economic activity. It is therefore necessary to give certainty to these industries and also to help them with corrective measures in the light of the current situation. We want to help this industry, also in energy efficiency and saving, and a regulatory framework is needed so that this technology can combine green reactivation and industrial recovery with technologies ready to use renewable gases and hydrogen, for industrial decarbonisation, which was not possible before, but is now possible. And it is that cogeneration remains the industry's great ally for decarbonisation, with renewable gas and hydrogen. That is why, Mr Baldoví, as you have rightly said, we urge you to look warmly at our amendments, even though we understand your specific position on some, which we also appreciate positively, as it cannot be otherwise. You mentioned that the European Commission - it is true - can make state aid more flexible, as it certainly did with Germany. I therefore believe that this is the way forward if we want this industry not to be displaced and competitive, at least in front of its European counterparts. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES_2022-12-21-CD221221.u355	Sweep on, good afternoon. This motion touches on a key aspect of the administration's relations with the citizenry, a crucial issue such as communication with the people to whom the administration owes itself. It refers to something as essential as guaranteeing the right of people to receive clear and accessible information, because this is a right of the people and it is a duty of the administrations to the citizenry. Moreover, it is crucial, because the consequences arising from the lack of clarity and difficulties in the processes of relations with the administrations become two very important problems, very important at all times but more in these times that we live in. The difficulty of relating to the Administration becomes the loss of confidence in the institutions and also leads to the violation of the rights of all persons by being unable to understand and access the information and services of the Administration. We already said last week that there is much talk about what is happening in the bank, with the elimination of face-to-face attention and the increase in online operations, but this is also happening in the Administration, and more and more. More and more, any kind of procedure must be done in a telematic way, including applications for aid and benefits such as minimum living income; aid for the poorest who, in many cases, are not processed by real impossibility by having to do so necessarily via the Internet. Although a computer can be accessed, what is also happening is that the procedures for accessing the subsidies, aid and services offered, and that they represent a lever of change to combat situations that may cause vulnerability, are neither clear nor accessible nor easy to request. It's not that we say it, it's that anyone who's tried to do a telematics procedure with the Public Administration knows the tortuous path of difficulty that opens up before him. Nor is it simply a matter of perceptions, because there are also studies that testify to it. One of them, the most recent I've found, is entitled 'Are the digital procedures clear?' of the company Prodigioso Volcano. The first conclusion of this report is that 83 % of the citizens leave some of the administration's digital procedures on average because I insist that 83 % of the population do not understand it. The second conclusion is that the main difficulty expressed by citizens in doing administrative procedures telematically is that they cannot resolve their doubts. They can't do it because they can't contact someone, because there's no one to contact; offices and closed doors, there's no one on the other side and there's not even a phone to call. In addition, there are no instructions, no detailed steps and the few indications are plagued with technicalities and terms that are incomprehensible to the vast majority of people. There are many problems detected. Of course, the technological capabilities of all people and all sectors must be advanced, but it is clear that the times between this digital development must be matched, to which we will inevitably have to add the population and the obligation that must be demanded of the Administration to facilitate communication with citizens. I think it is necessary to repeat that citizen care is a nuclear element and is a right of citizenship, so for people who do not have sufficient knowledge or the necessary technological equipment, the Administration has to establish channels that allow citizen care in an efficient way and, to do so, among other issues, face-to-face care should be guaranteed. Therefore, in this motion, we propose that access to information from the Administration be ensured to all citizens through adequate face-to-face attention; that the possibility of face-to-face processing and management be included, through non-telematic options, for all aids and applications to the Public Administration, including primarily requests for minimum vital income, which affect those who have less accessibility to online procedures; that the scope of Internet networks be boosted to all places where they find difficulties; and that the applications and Internet portals used by the Public Administration be changed to more intuitive and simplified platforms. And, with regard to banks, we ask that face-to-face attention be ensured in the branches of banks and boxes, extending schedules and without any payment for it. In short, these are the measures that we are putting in place to alleviate this problem, which is current and real as life itself and which affects not only the elderly - they are not confused, ladies and gentlemen - but a large majority of the population that is at large with this unintelligible tide that for many people comes to be, today, the public administration. Eskerrik asko.
