ParlaMint-ES_2022-12-21-CD221221.u357	Thank you, President. In our parliamentary group we have always defended the digitalisation of the Administration as a very important opportunity to ensure more efficient processes in public management and also closer to citizens through the digital media. If a citizen can have all his documents, procedures, requests and complaints in his hand, the Administration is more accessible and the line of communication between the citizen and the institutions is more direct. However, we are also aware of the huge digital divide that exists. In many reports, such as a recent Amazon Web Services report, it is said that only 22% of Spanish companies use technologies such as cloud or artificial intelligence, that only 9% of them use big data to improve organizational business decisions and that the situation is also very worrying in SMEs. As far as citizenship is concerned, another of the existing problems that the report also points out is that of digital skills. Only 57% of the adult population has basic digital skills and in recent years this figure has not improved. This is a symptom of the problems in our digital sector and it is really surprising that four out of ten Spaniards do not have basic digital skills. I think that we should work on it and that we should focus more on it; it is something that the motion proposes - through courses and so on - and that we share. Logically, it also speaks of the motion for the pending subject of the digitalization of the Administration, which is undoubtedly one of the aspects on which the Administration has to focus its efforts more. It is necessary to combine the promotion and development of digital public services with training for the acquisition of basic digital skills, because, in our opinion, the guarantee of invisibility should be maintained, but not as a basic priority, but as a transitional tool. We believe that our country is failing to take advantage of the opportunity offered by European funds, because the millions of euros that are being invested in digitisation are not having a real impact on the preparation of the private sector or on the public. We share with the proposer group the need to bet on more intuitive solutions than the current ones, which are in most cases absolutely outrageous and provoke, as the spokesman has said, the abandonment of the process on many occasions. But I also have to say that we do not share either the first or the last point of the motion - which we have amended - which states that it is necessary to maintain face-to-face attention anywhere unconditionally. In the case of the first point, we believe that this solution, as we have said, can only be transitory as the new digital relationship systems develop and as the efforts to train citizens are carried out. It cannot be an objective in itself to maintain headquarters, timetables and staff in all types of services, because it would not be efficient in allocating resources, especially in view of the fact that there are sectors of the Administration in which staff are lacking, such as, for example, aid and application processing services. Pretending that everything is present is a well-intentioned ideal, but we consider it unrealistic and inappropriate to the current state of sociological reality. However, in the transitional period, the availability of basic social services in person must be guaranteed and protected, while promoting and providing adequate and, of course, free training for the use of the digital administration. At the last point of the motion, we do not agree that it is necessary to oblige private companies, in this case financial institutions, to maintain physical headquarters. Digitization is a trend that already exists and that is unstoppable; it is not something that must necessarily be reversed. We bet that banks can decide on the organization of their citizen care services in the way they see fit and that face-to-face care is seen as a competitive advantage. I am sure that, if financial institutions have an appropriate business strategy, they will not close offices in places where people are asking for these services. In addition, there are other mechanisms and tools, such as cashback - yesterday a GNP was approved on this driven by my group - which can also help cash services provided in places where there may not be a bank office. In short, and finally, we believe that the dichotomy presented by the proposing group is not real.

When Bertrand Russell came to Madrid to give a conference in the 1930s, he said that after a few decades, only three or four hours would be worked, because technological advances would make it possible to reduce the working day enormously. Did this happen? No, the opposite has happened: in the 21st century there are people, for example, in the hospitality sector, who work with infinite hours and in conditions of almost slavery. Bertrand Russell was wrong, and he was wrong not because he made a naive statement, but because there is a planned - and very well planned - strategy so that new technologies can be used to reduce and confiscate rights, to increase the concentration of wealth in few hands, and for a social regression and a worsening of the quality of life. In December 2020, the Council of Ministers approved the Plan for Connectivity and Digital Infrastructures of Society, Economy and Territories and the 5G Technology Impulse Strategy, the objectives of which are to further expand high-speed broadband coverage to 100 per cent of the population and contribute to the reduction of digital divides, among others. The Digital Spain 2025 agenda, updated to 2026, is the road map for this transformation of the country, by guaranteeing digital connectivity and also guaranteeing the digital rights of citizens. It is designed as a tool to act in three dimensions: infrastructure and technology,economy and people; and incorporates two new cross-cutting axes into the strategy, digital skills and digital rights. Digital Competencies, through the National Plan of Digital Competencies, the aim of which is to ensure that all people can interact, buy, make transactions and communicate with public administrations, autonomously, adequately and securely; ultimately, to promote digital inclusion. Digital rights, with the adoption of the Digital Rights Charter, which, without being normative in nature, provides a framework for guaranteeing the rights of citizens in the new digital reality and which sets out as its main objective to promote and facilitate access to, use and training of, digital environments for all groups. Aware of this reality, the Government of Spain has put in place concrete plans and measures for certain sectors: the plan to strengthen face care - the Government has expressly approved a programme aimed at the attention of the offices of information and citizen care and registration of persons over 65 years of age, without the need for a prior appointment, through the implementation of plans to strengthen face care - to ensure telephone care through 060, which attends 24 hours a day directly; the production of videos and leaflets about the electronic administration, and also through SEPE, which throughout the territory, in eight autonomous communities and twenty-six provinces, is deploying a digital training programme to women included in the PROFEA programme and which aims to reach 180,000 women. The commitment of the Government of Spain to the invisibility of the provision of public services is undeniable, but we cannot forget the reality of the public services that this Government inherited as a result of the disastrous personnel policies in the years of the People's Party's government, with the cuts that this Government is reversing by approving the largest public employment offers: almost 144,000 public jobs called since Pedro Sánchez is president of the Government, more than twice as much as those approved by the People's Party in its seven years of government. We are therefore talking about three digital divides, which have also evolved according to technologies, which have been incorporated into society: the one that distinguishes between people connected to technological devices and those who are not, the one that distinguishes the difficulties faced by people in using new technologies once they have achieved access to them, and the one that focuses on the unequal opportunities that people have to take advantage of precisely these technologies. ParlaMint-ES_2022-12-21-CD221221.u390	The honourable Member, please, I ask you, on the basis of Rule 102 of the Rules of Procedure, to stick to the question, and you know what the question is. We're talking about a digital divide. I ask you, please, to stick to the matter. Thank you. [[Protests]]
ParlaMint-ES_2022-12-21-CD221221.u391	On the other hand, the Group of the European People's Party is committed to digitisation, because we know that it is an unstoppable process that improves and facilitates the lives of citizens. But like any transformative process, if it is not done well, as it is happening, it can leave people who for various reasons - age, socio-economic situation or educational level - do not have the same learning tools as the rest of the population. This situation, contrary to what PSOE partners propose, is not reversed by paralysing digitization and returning to traditional face-to-face services; this situation is reversed with appropriate policy and resources. There must be face-to-face attention, but not to carry out the processes in a traditional way, but to accompany in this learning process. Why condemn a life without the advantages of technology to a part of the population that cannot have the same benefits as the rest? Ladies and gentlemen of the PSOE, a person who first comes to the digital world needs much more than a seven-hour course that will fatten the statistics of his National Plan of Digital Competences; they are wasting Europe's funds and it will not do any good. By the way, it is a plan that has serious problems of coordination and duplication. The ministries of Digital Transformation, Employment and Equality are giving the same courses with the same content precisely to the same women; it is shameful what they are doing. Ladies and gentlemen, there is no need for citizens to know which body is helping, in which ministry or in which tab they need to tap on the complicated webs of ministries. They have to have a single window where only the information the citizen needs is shown. Today, technology, with artificial intelligence and conversational assistants, would directly allow the citizen to tell the Administration what it needs, and the Administration would demonstrate precisely what it needs without giving it those complicated websites and so much information. The training model has to be a permanent service of care and advice, because the needs of this population without digital skills are continuous and varied: from the handling of the mobile phone to asking for a medical appointment, aids of minimum vital income, online banking or any administrative procedure, whether of SEPE, of the regional government or of its council, but also with companies, such as downloading the bills of light, water or knowing the conditions of their telephone contract. Do you think, gentlemen of the PSOE, that you learn with seven hours in a course? Our model, for example, is the model of the Junta de Andalucía, where Juanma Moreno is developing digital training centers in every small town - we already have almost 800 - where digital mentors with indefinite contract - percent, paid by the Junta and the deputies - advise in all the services that the population needs, where every citizen learns at their own pace and where, moreover, citizens feel accompanied in this increasingly digital world. These Centers are being completed with online banking services, and have ATMs that are serviced by videoconferencing so that users can do the same things they used to do in their branches. They also have tele-assistance services and, of course, have all the technological means to do so. Gentlemen of the PSOE, it is urgent that you remember again that you are here to govern for all Spaniards and to seek solutions, such as these I explain to you, that help people with fewer opportunities, and not to think about how to get their government partners out of jail, how to undermine the constitutional principles that we all gave ourselves, or how to confront the Spaniards with each other. Gentlemen of the PSOE, when you remember all this again, maybe it's late. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
